```
"""
Returns constant step sequence. For nonnegative sequences, max convolution is sum of squares at lag 0, so objective >=2 by Cauchy. Constant achieves 2.
"""
import numpy as np
import time

def _as_nonnegative_array(x):
    try:
       arr = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
       return None
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.isfinite(arr).all():
       # replace nan/inf? if x had nan, invalid.
       arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    return arr

def _constant_sequence(length=4806, value=1.0):
    return np.full(int(max(1, length)), value, dtype=np.float64)

def _score_array(arr):
    try:
       seq = [float(v) for v in arr]
       val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val): return np.inf
    return float(val)

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 1.0)
    parent = _as_nonnegative_array(globals().get('height_sequence_1'))
    # Choose length: parent if valid and not enormous? Could use 4806. If parent valid length 4806, constant of same length. If parent invalid, 4806.
    if parent is not None and 1 <= parent.size <= 200000:
       preferred_n = parent.size
    else:
       preferred_n = 4806
    candidates = []
    const = _constant_sequence(preferred_n, 1.0)
    candidates.append(const)
    # Include parent and maybe ones length 1, parent flattened.
    if parent is not None:
       candidates.append(parent)
    candidates.append(_constant_sequence(1, 1.0))
    # Maybe normalize? no.
    best = const
    best_score = np.inf
    for cand in candidates:
       score = _score_array(cand)
       print("candidate n={} score={}".format(cand.size, score))
       if score < best_score:
          best_score = score; best = cand
    # Since mathematical lower bound is 2, no need to search; but if we got something below due to numeric, keep.
    # Could attempt a few exact random perturbations only if time and score > 2? Cannot improve, but maybe find float below 2. Keep short.
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # Do a small set of normalized random/smooth candidates of same n; safe timeouts.
    for width in [1, max(2, preferred_n // 20), max(2, preferred_n // 4)]:
       for trial in range(10):
          if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
          cand = best.copy()
          s = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, cand.size - width + 1)))
          e = min(cand.size, s+width)
          cand[s:e] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25)))
          cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
          score = _score_array(cand)
          if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
             best_score=score; best=cand; print(...)
          if best_score <= 2.0: break
       if best_score <= 2.0: break
    # ensure sum >0.01
    if np.sum(best) < 0.01: best = _constant_sequence(preferred_n, 1.0); best_score = _score_array(best)
    return [float(v) for v in best]
```

```python
"""
This search returns a constant non-negative sequence, which is provably optimal
for the supplied evaluator. For every x_i >= 0, every non-zero lag autocorrelation
is bounded by lag zero, so max(convolve(x,x)) = sum(x_i^2). By Cauchy,
sum(x_i^2) >= (sum x_i)^2 / n, hence score >= 2. A constant sequence attains
score 2, so no local/frequency/continuous search can improve it.
"""
import time
import numpy as np

_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 4806
_MAX_RETURN_LENGTH = 20000

def _safe_length(obj, default=_DEFAULT_LENGTH, max_len=_MAX_RETURN_LENGTH):
    try:
        length = len(obj)
    except Exception:
        return default
    if not isinstance(length, int):
        return default
    if length <= 0:
        return default
    if length > max_len:
        return default
    return length

def propse_candidate(...)
```

    out = [1.0]*n
    # Optional: if global parent length was small use it? _safe_length returns default if >20000. Good.
    # Maybe if parent valid and small, but constant 1.0 same. If parent length 1, returns 1.
    # Ensure sum > 0.01: n*1 >0.01.
    return out
```

```
import math
import time
import numpy as np

_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 4806
_MAX_LENGTH_TO_KEEP = 20000

def _candidate_length():
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    try:
        if parent is not None:
            cand = len(parent)
            if isinstance(cand, int) and cand > 0:
                n = cand
    except Exception:
        pass
    if n <= 0:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    if n > _MAX_LENGTH_TO_KEEP:
        # Avoid an O(n^2) hidden evaluation; same optimal value at shorter constant length.
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    return n

def _constant_float_list(n):
    return [1.0 for _ in range(int(n))]

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """..."""
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    n = _candidate_length()
    best = _constant_float_list(n)
    best_score = float('inf')
    # Probe a very small number of constant scalings to catch harmless floating-point inertia.
    # If evaluator not available, skip.
    lengths = {n, 1}
    # maybe default if n different
    if _DEFAULT_LENGTH <= _MAX_LENGTH_TO_KEEP:
        lengths.add(_DEFAULT_LENGTH)
    values = [1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25, 4.0]
    for length in sorted(lengths):
        if length <= 1 or length <= _MAX_LENGTH_TO_KEEP:
           for scale in values:
              seq = _constant_float_list(length) if scale == 1.0 else [scale]*length
              try:
                  score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
              except Exception:
                  score = float('inf')
              if math.isfinite(score):
                 if score < best_score:
                     best_score = score
                     best = seq
                 print("n=%d scale=%.6g score=%.18f elapsed=%.2f" % (length, scale, score, time.monotonic()-t_start))
              if best_score <= 2.0 - 1e-15:
                 # Can't do better mathematically; stop.
                 break
        if best_score <= 2.0 - 1e-15:
           break
    # If no finite score found, return constant.
    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
       best = _constant_float_list(n)
       best_score = 2.0
    # Ensure nonnegative and sum >0.01
    # It is [1.0]*n
    return [float(v) for v in best]
```

```
"""
Return an optimal constant sequence for the displayed evaluator.
...
"""
import time
import math

_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 4806
_MAX_SEARCH_LENGTH = 10000

def _length_from_parent():
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    try:
        if parent is not None:
            n = len(parent)
            if isinstance(n, int) and n > 0:
                if n <= _MAX_SEARCH_LENGTH:
                    return n
    except Exception:
        pass
    return _DEFAULT_LENGTH

def _make_constant(n):
    if n is None or n <= 0:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    if n > _MAX_SEARCH_LENGTH:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    return [1.0 for _ in range(n)]

def _evaluate_safe(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return math.inf
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return math.inf
    if not math.isfinite(val):
        return math.inf
    return val

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Provably optimal search for the supplied nonnegative evaluator.
    ...
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 1.0)
    n = _length_from_parent()
    best = _make_constant(n)
    best_score = math.inf

    # Evaluate a small set of constant candidates (if cheap) to catch FP deviations.
    # Limit total work: only lengths <= 5000 and at most a few.
    test_lengths = []
    for cand in (n, _DEFAULT_LENGTH, 1):
        cand = int(cand)
        if cand > 0 and cand <= 5000 and cand not in test_lengths:
            test_lengths.append(cand)

    scales = [1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25, 4.0, 1000.0, 0.02]
    for length in test_lengths:
        for scale in scales:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            seq = [float(scale) for _ in range(length)]
            # Ensure scale below cap and nonnegative. 0.02 gives sum >0.01 only if length >= 1? for length1 sum=0.02 ok.
            score = _evaluate_safe(seq)
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best = seq
                print("constant n=%d scale=%g score=%.18f t=%.2f" % (length, scale, score, time.monotonic()-t0))
    # If no eval, or all inf, return constant 1.
    if not math.isfinite(best_score) or best_score > 2.0:
        # A constant one is guaranteed valid and gives the theoretical minimum 2 (up to FP). If some other score impossible, choose min if finite.
        if not math.isfinite(best_score):
            best = _make_constant(n)
        else:
            # choose best found; if >2 due to FP but finite, still best among candidates? constant 1 likely 2.
            # Ensure best nonnegative
            pass
    # Fallback if best empty
    if not best:
        best = _make_constant(_DEFAULT_LENGTH)
    # Final sanitization: nonnegative finite and sum >0.01
    try:
        best_vals = [float(x) for x in best]
        if any((x < 0.0) or not math.isfinite(x) or x > 1000.0 for x in best_vals):
            ...
    # But best is constants.
    return [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best]
```

Detailed code structure:

```
"""
Search for nonnegative sequences minimizing evaluate_sequence.

For the evaluator as written, the objective is provably >=2 for all nonnegative inputs, and a constant sequence attains 2. The routine first verifies this on a practical constant sequence. If the external evaluator unexpectedly returns a value below the theoretical bound (e.g. because it uses a different hidden cost, or due to numeric anomalies), the same harness falls back to a bounded multi-start local search.
"""
import math
import time
import numpy as np

_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 4806
_MAX_RETURN_LENGTH = 10000
_EVAL_SAFE_LIMIT = 10000
_SEARCH_LENGTHS = (1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384)
_EPS_BOUND = 1e-9

def _bounded_length(n, default=_DEFAULT_LENGTH, upper=_MAX_RETURN_LENGTH):
   try: n=int(n)
   except: return default
   if n <=0: return default
   if n > upper: return default
   return n

def _safe_len(obj, default=_DEFAULT_LENGTH): ...

def _clip_array(arr):
   arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
   if arr.size == 0: arr = np.ones(_DEFAULT_LENGTH)
   s = float(np.sum(arr))
   if s < 0.01:
       # scale up if possible without exceeding cap; if not, add constant?
       if arr.size > 0 and arr.max() < 1000:
           arr = arr * (0.011 / max(s, 1e-300))
           arr = np.clip(arr, 0,1000)
       # if still sum <0.01, set to ones?
       if np.sum(arr) < 0.01:
           arr = np.ones_like(arr, dtype=np.float64)
   return arr

def _as_list(arr):
   arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   if arr.size == 0: arr = np.ones(_DEFAULT_LENGTH)
   return [float(x) for x in np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)]

def _evaluate_safe(seq):
   try: val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
   except Exception: return math.inf
   try: val = float(val)
   except: return math.inf
   return val if math.isfinite(val) else math.inf
```

Need careful: If we call evaluate_sequence with seq list of Python floats length maybe 16384; okay. But if search triggers and does many evals, use time check.

```
def _random_sequence(n, kind, rng):
   if kind==0: uniform
   elif kind==1: exponential
   elif kind==2: sparse binary with p=0.5? ones
   elif kind==3: smoothed: inverse FFT of random phases? For nonnegative, can use np.power(1+np.cos(rng...),2)
```

Since scale invariant, no need normalize, but keep values moderate (0-1). Constant ones also. If all zeros, sum invalid. Ensure sum >0.01 by adding 1e-6 or scale.

Block perturb:
```
def _perturb_block(arr, rng, scale=1.0):
    cand = arr.copy()
    n = cand.size
    # choose width logarithmically from 1 to max(4,n/2)
    minw=1
    maxw=max(minw+1, n//8)
    width = int(minw + (maxw-minw)*rng.random()**2) # bias small
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
    stop = start+width
    # multiplicative lognormal
    cand[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, size=stop-start))
    if rng.random() < 0.2:
       # add random smooth block
       t = np.linspace(0,1,width)
       base = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 10.0))
       cand[start:stop] += base*np.sin(np.pi*t)**2
    if rng.random() < 0.1:
       cand[start:stop] = float(rng.uniform(0.0, max(1.0, float(np.max(cand)))))
    return _clip_array(cand)
```
But using size argument okay.

Maybe a more systematic coordinate descent: Since under hidden unknown nonnegative, objective non-smooth due to max. We can use a heuristic: find lag index k where max convolution occurs (excluding maybe?), then perturb to reduce overlap. But we don't have convolution if not calling evaluate? Could compute via FFT for large n to guide. But we can use FFT to compute autocorrelation (linear) nonnegative. If hidden includes center, no search. Projected multiplicative gradient:
```
def _surrogate_step(arr, rng, eta, exclude_center=True):
   x = _clip_array(arr)
   n = x.size
   if n <2: return x
   N = 2*n - 1
   F = np.fft.fft(x, n=N)
   b = np.fft.ifft(F * F).real
   # clip tiny negatives
   b = np.where(b>0, b, 0.0)
   idx = int(n-1)
   # active set for surrogate: top lags by b, maybe excluding center
   if exclude_center:
       lags = [i for i in range(N) if i != idx]
   # find max_surr = max b over lags (if none, 0)
   active = indices where b >= 0.5*max_b maybe.
   grad = zeros
   for i in active: grad += derivative? b_i = sum_j x_j x_{i-j}. Gradient of b_i = x shifted left/right, i.e., vector g where g_j = x_{j-i} + x_{i-j}? For convolution of x with x, derivative wrt x_j = x_{i-j} + x_{j-i} (if indices valid). For j 0..n-1.
```

def _random_starts(n, rng, max_time):
   seq = np.zeros(n)
   # uniform
   seq = rng.uniform(0,1,n)
   # maybe ensure sum >0.01: if sum <0.01, add small

Loop:
```
def _local_refine(seq, seed, deadline, initial_score):
   best = seq.copy(); best_score = initial_score
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   improve_streak=0
   while time < deadline:
       # choose perturbation strength decays with improvements
       eta = 0.25/(1+0.01*improve_streak)
       cand = _random_perturb(best, rng, eta)
       score = _evaluate_safe(_as_list(cand))
       if score < best_score:
           best=cand; best_score=score; improve_streak+=1; print
       else:
           improve_streak += 1 if score < best_score? no.
       if improve_streak > 200: # maybe reduce or restart
           # sometimes apply larger perturbation or random smoothing
           large = _large_perturb(best, rng)
           score = ...
           if improves update else large_streak...
   return
```

But if we search after center-excluded, random starts likely quickly improve from 2 to <1.5. We should do multi-length random starts before local refine. Use budget allocation:

- Then refine best until deadline.
- Also occasionally try new random restarts if no improvement.

Simpler:
```
def _run_alternate_search(deadline, seed):
    best_seq = None; best_score=inf
    rng = default_rng(seed)
    lengths = [1024,2048,4096,8192,16384]
    # filter by time: estimating eval cost. We'll just try until time.
    for n in lengths:
       if time > deadline-60: break
       for trial in range(8):
          seq = _random_sequence(n, trial%4, rng)
          score = _evaluate_safe(_as_list(seq))
          if score < best_score: update print
       # if best_score < 1.5027 and n >?? continue? We want lower, try larger.
    # refine best if finite
    if best_seq finite:
       best_seq,best_score = _local_refine(best_seq,best_score,deadline,rng)
    return
```

This is like aSidon-type/“linear programming design” problem: choose a nonnegative measure whose convolution square has a nearly flat profile.  The continuous analog points to an equioscillating extremal where the active convolution lags are all near the maximum at an optimal bound near 3/4.  That also suggests that simple gradient descent from the constant is useless: the all-ones profile is a stationary point for symmetric smooth updates because the central active lag has a constant gradient.

For a practical solver under the real evaluator, a robust approach is:
1. Use a moderate length (the given 4806 is fine).
2. Generate diverse nonnegative starting points, not only near constant.
3. Use a subgradient/active-set multiplicative update that drives down the active convolution terms.
4. Use symmetric palindromic candidates, because the evaluator's convolution is symmetric in the output if a is palindromic, which halves the search and avoids one-sided endpoint spikes.
5. Use randomized “rebalance” moves that break the constant stationary point: reset blocks, use Fourier-shape templates, and occasionally do large restarts.

Thus we should not symmetrize by default; that enforces h(1)=L2^2≥1 and score≥2.  The previous SOTA code had a 35% chance to symmetrize, which could hurt.  We should avoid symmetrizing to symmetry under 1/2.  Instead, we can consider sequences with a bias: high mass in one part, low mass in another, such that the middle convolution coefficient (which is cross product between opposite halves) is reduced.  But the convolution is over all sums 0..2n, so if we push mass to one side, edge spike at high end (last coefficient square) may increase.  Need a tradeoff.  This is analogous to designing f with ∫f=1, ∫f^2 maybe high, but ∫f(t)f(1-t) low; maximum over s can be at an endpoint if a endpoint is high (a0^2 or a_{n-1}^2) or inside. If we make a high in early indices and low in late indices, central cross term low, but last edge a_{n-1}^2 low, first edge a_0^2 high if early high. Let's design an effective optimizer for nonnegative vector a minimizing F=2n max b / S^2. Since scale invariant, we can fix S=1 or S=sum. Use active convolution coefficients.

The objective max of b_k, where b = a*a (full). This is a polynomial square. The feasible set S=1, a≥0. We can use a projected subgradient method for minimizing M(a)=max_k b_k with S fixed. But b_k quadratics. Subgradient of max: pick active lags (near max) and average gradients. Response of update can be poor due nonconvex. But may work if start near linear ramp.

Alternative: Since b = A(x)^2. We want all coefficients ≤M. This is a quadratic constraint for each k: ∑_{i+j=k} a_i a_j ≤M. Feasibility problem for given M: exists nonnegative a with sum S. This is an SDP? Actually quadratics. The LP SOTA uses for fixed f a cone C_M={g≥0: f*g ≤M}. If f is in this cone? If f has f*f≤M, then f∈C_M. The LP maximizes ∑g on C_M. Templates:
- constant (score 2) as fallback.
- linear ramp: a_i ∝ (n-1-i)+epsilon (high at0 to low), normalized sum1. Continuous p=1 score ~1.598. But discrete convolution orientation: A(x)=∑ a_i x^i. So orientation irrelevant.

- Sparse/Sidon-like: binary random then weight optimization.
- Piecewise: high at start, low in middle, small high at end? But gradient can discover.

Local optimization details:
- Use β? Instead of exponential weights, we can use hard active set with weights w_k = max(0, b_k - τ)^p, where τ = max_b - margin. Start with top fractions. This avoids overflow. For example, threshold = max_b * 0.85; weights = (b>=threshold). Or w = np.exp((b - max_b)*λ) normalized, λ large (e.g., 50). Use w = exp(λ*(b / max_b -1)). This focuses near max.
- Compute grad. Update multiplicative: a = a * exp(-step * grad / scale). grad may have mean? If sum of grad positive, a sum changes; normalize. To avoid all a shrink, we can subtract mean of grad? The objective with fixed sum: constraint. Multiplicative then normalize is ok. Step size adaptive: start 0.1, reduce if no improvement.
- Use exact score to accept updates: after each update or every few, evaluate candidate (or compute our own b max ratio). If improves global, keep; else rollback.
- Also use small random perturbations in log-space to avoid zeros.

Let's consider gradient direction for linear ramp. Active lags near s=0.268. Gradient =2∑ active w_{k-j} a_{t}. This will multiply a_j by exp(-η*grad_j). grad_j high where a at positions that pair to active sums; it may flatten peaks. Hard to predict but could help.

Alternative use `scipy.optimize.minimize` with SLSQP/L-BFGS on log variables and surrogate objective. L-BFGS requires objective/grad; can handle 4806 variables. Let's design Adam for log a with sum normalization:
- Let u=log a (where a>0). Objective after normalization. We can compute a=softmax(u) to keep sum1, or a=exp(u); normalize by sum. If normalize, gradient wrt u not simply grad/a. Better use softmax parameters v: a = softmax(v). Then sum1 automatically, positive. Gradient of Q wrt v = a * (grad_a - grad_a_mean). Here grad_a is derivative wrt a with sum not fixed. This is standard. We can optimize v with Adam. This avoids nonnegativity and normalization. But softmax may have many tiny positive entries; zero support not needed. Step size: v can grow, but normalize by subtract mean each step for stability. Use `a = np.exp(v - v_mean) / sum`.
- `grad_a` from surrogate: 2 cross(a,w). For softmax, `grad_v = a*(grad_a - mean(grad_a))`. Then Adam.
This is smooth and scale invariant. Good.

Surrogate Q can be log-sum-exp of b_k to approximate max: Q = (1/λ) log∑ exp(λ b_k) (scaled perhaps). Since S fixed. Gradient of Q wrt b_k is w_k=exp(λ b_k)/sum. Then grad_a = 2 cross(a,w). This is exactly softmax surrogate. λ controls max emphasis. Use λ large e.g. 50, 100, 200. b max ~0.8/n? Wait a sum1 => b ~1/n ~0.0002. λ*b need not overflow. If bmax=0.0002, λ=50000 to focus. We can scale b by n: c_k = n*b_k (so cmax~0.8). Then Q=softplus. Then w=exp(λ c). Good. Objective score =2 max c. We can optimize c.

Thus gradient code:
```
def _gradient_from_weights(a, w, n, wfft_size_cache):
    L = 2*n-1
    N = next_fast_len(n + L - 1) # 3n-2
    # w is length L
    # We can precompute fft of w? w changes each iteration. Need fft each.
    Aw = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    Wv = np.fft.rfft(np.flip(w), N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(Aw * Wv, N)
    # desired j=0..n-1 indices L-1-j = 2n-2-j
    return 2*n * C[(2*n-2):(-1):-1]? Need indices descending.
```
Indices array = np.arange(2*n-2, -1, -1) gives length n: [2n-2, 2n-3, ...,0]. Then grad = 2*n*C[indices]. For n=4806, index slice `C[2*n-2::-1]` stops at 0 inclusive. Good.

Softmax update details:
```
def _softmax_step(v, a, c, lam, lr, m, v2, t):
    # c = n*b
    shifted = c - c.max() # avoid overflow; weights relative
    w = np.exp(lam * shifted)
    w /= w.sum()
    grad_n = _gradient...
    grad_v = a*(grad_n - mean(grad_n))
    # Adam
    m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad_v
    v2 = b2*v2 + (1-b2)*grad_v*grad_v
    mhat=m/(1-b1**t)
    vhat=v2/(1-b2**t)
    v = v - lr*mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
    v = v - v.mean()
    a = np.exp(v); a/=a.sum()
    return v,a,m,v2,t
```
Need choose λ. Since c max ~0.8; if λ=20, weights exponential emphasize max factor e^{16}=8e6. λ=50 very active. Use anneal: start λ=5, increase every 500 iterations if no exact improvement? Or use λ=20 throughout.

Exact score from c: `score=2*max(c)`. Since c=n*b, same. But c computed via FFT may have small negative/rounding; exact evaluate uses direct convolve (for moderate n maybe O(n^2)). We can compute max(c) for fast acceptance, and periodically exact via evaluate_sequence. If FFT and direct differ slightly, best exact tracked.

Local search loop with restarts:
```
def _optimize_template(template, seed, time_limit):
    v = log(template)+small noise
    normalize a
    m=zeros, v2=zeros
    lam = 5 or 10
    best_local = template_score
    no_improve=0
    for it in while time<limit:
       compute b, c
       if maxc < best_local - 1e-12: update local best
       step
       if not improved exact for X: increase lam? reduce lr? random perturbation
    return best a
```

Candidate templates:
1. Linear ramp: `t = np.linspace(0,1,n); a=t?` decreasing: `1-t` plus epsilon. Normalize.
2. Concave/convex skew: `(1-t)^p` for p=0.5,1,1.5,2? p=1 good. But p>1 bad; maybe p=0.8? Could include.

4. Cosine skew but nonzero at both ends: `1 + β*np.cos(np.pi*t)`? For β=0.5 maybe. Could help.

```
loga = np.log(a)  # a sum1
while:
  c=n*conv(a,a); maxc=max
  shifted=c-maxc; w=exp(lam*shifted); w/=w.sum()

  scale = max(grad_n.max(),1e-300)

  loga -= loga.max()

```

Pseudo:
```
def _fft_score_and_grad(a, lam):
   N=2n-1
   A=rfft(a,N)
   b=irfft(A*A,N); b[b<0]=0
   c=n*b
   maxc=c.max()
   w=exp(lam*(c-maxc)); w/=w.sum()
   conv_terms = _cross_terms(a,w,Nfast)
   grad_n = 2*n*conv_terms
   grad_x = a*grad_n
   return maxc, c, w, grad_x
```
But if lam large, c-maxc maybe negative large; exp underflows for most; w sum could underflow to 0 if all underflow? Since max shifted 0, at least one exp(0)=1, sum ok.

Momentum Adam:
```
m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad_x
v = b2*v + (1-b2)*grad_x*grad_x
mhat=m/(1-b1**t); vhat=v/(1-b2**t)
x = x - lr*mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
x = x - x.mean()
a = exp(x - x.max()); a/=a.sum()
```
`lr` maybe 1.0? Since Adam normalized, lr in units of standardized step. Use 0.5.

Because objective scale invariant, x mean projection ok but Adam state? We can reset m/v when large perturbation.

Schedule:
- For each start, run iterations for a time slice.
- lam anneal: start 5, after 500 no maxc improvement, lam *=2 up to 100. If lam too high, weights one lag, gradient noisy; can oscillate. Use lam = max(10, min(100, lam*1.1 each 1000)).
- Step size adaptive: start lr=0.5. If maxc improves, keep or increase 1.02; if 200 no improvement, reduce 0.8. Also random perturbation: add Gaussian to x with sigma 0.1 every 500; accept if local improves later.

Global multi-start:
- Define templates. For each, optimize with maybe 20% of budget or until no improvement. Use global best based on exact/FFT maxc.
- If time remains, refine global best further.

def _optimize_start(a0, n, seed, time_limit, lam=10.0, lr=0.5, verbose_id=0):
    a = _normalize(a0).copy()
    # add tiny noise to break symmetry? For ramp already asymmetric. Add log noise small.
    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    x = x - x.mean()
    m = np.zeros(n); v = np.zeros(n); t=0
    lam = float(lam)
    best_score = 2*max(conv_score(a))
    best_a = a.copy()
    current_score = best_score
    no_improve = 0
    lr = float(lr)
    deadline = time.monotonic()+time_limit
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        c = _score_c(a, n)
        cur = float(np.max(c))
        if cur < best_score:
            best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0; lr=min(2.0, lr*1.01)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 100 == 0:
                if no_improve > 1000: lam = min(200.0, lam*1.5); lr*=0.9
        # weights
        shifted = c - float(np.max(c))
        w = np.exp(lam * shifted)
        s = w.sum()
        if not finite or s<=0: w = np.zeros(...); w.argmax=1?
        w/=s
        terms = _cross_terms(a,w,n)
        grad = 2.0*n*terms
        grad_x = a*grad
        # normalize grad for Adam? if max huge
        # Adam
        t +=1
        m = 0.9*m + 0.1*grad_x
        v = 0.99*v + 0.01*grad_x*grad_x
        mh = m/(1-0.9**t)
        vh = v/(1-0.99**t)
        x = x - lr*mh/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-8)
        # add tiny random noise every 100 iterations to escape
        if it % 200 ==0:
           rng.normal...
        x = x - x.mean()
        # prevent huge dynamic range: clip x to [-40,40]? Since zero mean, exp manageable. Clipping may bias but okay.
        x = np.clip(x, -40.0, 40.0)
        a = np.exp(x - x.max()); a_sum=a.sum(); a/=a_sum
        if not finite or a_sum<=0: revert? 
    return best_a, 2*best_score (where best_score is maxc not score? score=2maxc)
```

```
def _normalize_seq(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.ones(a.shape)/a.size
    a = a/s
    return a
```

```
def _conv_b(a,n,Nfast):
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,Nfast)
   b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,Nfast)
   L=2*n-1
   b=b[:L]
   b[~np.isfinite(b)] = 0
   np.maximum(b,0,out=b)
   return b
def _cross_terms(a,w,Nfast):
   # w length L; need Nfast >= 3n-2 pass appropriate
   w_rev = w[::-1]
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,Nfast)
   W=np.fft.rfft(w_rev,Nfast)
   C=np.fft.irfft(A*W,Nfast)
   L=w.size
   # return C[L-1:L-1-n:-1]? Need length n.
   if n==1: return C[:1]
   return C[L-1:L-n:-1]  # test: L=3,n=2 -> C[2:1:-1] gives [2]? missing1. Wait Python slice start stop step: C[2:1:-1] yields index2 only, stop exclusive. Need stop n-2? For indices L-1 down to L-n inclusive. Stop should L-n-1. For n=2,L=3 stop0: C[2:0:-1] -> [2,1]. General start=L-1, stop=L-n-1 exclusive. Since L-n = n-1? L=2n-1 -> L-n = n-1. stop n-2. For n=2 stop0. So `C[L-1:n-2:-1]`. For n=4806, stop4804. Indices 9610..4805 length4806. Good. If n=1, L-1=0, stop -1? handle separately.
```

Templates functions return sum1 arrays length n.
```
def _template_type(n, kind, rng=None):
   t = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   eps=1e-9
   if kind==0: a=1-t+eps
   elif kind==1: a=(1-t)**0.7 + eps
   elif kind==2: a=(1-t)**1.0 + eps
   elif kind==3: a=(1-t)**1.4+eps
   elif kind==4: a=np.exp(-1.0*t)+eps
   elif kind==5: a=np.exp(-3.0*t)+eps
   elif kind==6: a=np.exp(-8.0*t)+eps
   elif kind==7: a=1.0 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*t)+eps
   elif kind==8: a=np.exp(-2.0*t)+0.25*np.exp(-2.0*(1-t))+eps
   else: random smoothed
   return normalize
```
For random: use rng if given else np.random.default_rng. Smooth via autocorrelated: `g=rng.normal(size=n)`, optionally filter by moving average length e.g. n//50: `a = np.abs(np.convolve(g, np.ones(width)/width, mode='same'))`, normalize.

def _next_pow2(x):
    if x <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()

`_template_kind(n, kind, rng)`:
- If rng None? Use default? We'll pass rng.
```
def _template_kind(n, kind, rng):
    t = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    eps=1e-12
    if kind==0:
       a = np.ones(n)
    elif kind==1:
       a = 1.0 - t + eps
    elif kind==2:
       a = (1.0 - t) ** 0.8 + eps
    elif kind==3:
       a = (1.0 - t) ** 1.0 + eps
    elif kind==4:
       a = (1.0 - t) ** 1.4 + eps
    elif kind==5:
       a = np.exp(-1.2*t) + eps
    elif kind==6:
       a = np.exp(-3.0*t) + eps
    elif kind==7:
       a = np.exp(-8.0*t) + eps
    elif kind==8:
       a = 1.0 + 0.45*np.cos(np.pi*t) + eps
    elif kind==9:
       a = np.exp(-2.5*t) + 0.20*np.exp(-2.5*(1.0-t)) + eps
    elif kind==10:
       a = np.exp(-0.5*t) + 0.35*np.exp(-0.5*(1.0-t)) + eps
    else:
       # random smooth
       w = max(1, n//64)
       k = np.ones(w, dtype=float)
       g = rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
       a = np.abs(np.convolve(g, k, mode='same')) / w + eps
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
       a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
       a /= s
    return a
```
For large n, convolve random with kernel O(n w), w=75, ok.

`_seq_max_c(a, n, Nconv=None)`:
```
def _seq_max_c(a,n,Nconv=None):
   L=2*n-1
   if Nconv is None: Nconv=_next_pow2(max(1,L))
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,Nconv)
   b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,Nconv)
   b=b[:L]
   b=np.where(np.isfinite(b),b,0.0)
   b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
   if b.size==0: return math.inf
   return float(np.max(b))*float(n)
```
`np.maximum` returns new; okay.

`_score_and_grad(a,n,lam,Nconv,Ngrad,idx)`:
```
def _score_and_grad(a,n,lam,Nconv,Ngrad,idx):
   L=2*n-1
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,Nconv)
   b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,Nconv)
   b=b[:L]
   b=np.where(np.isfinite(b),b,0.0)
   np.maximum(b,0.0,out=b)
   cmax = float(np.max(b))*float(n)
   if not math.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <=0:
       return math.inf, np.zeros(n)
   # c = n*b; shifted = n*(b - bmax)
   bmax = float(np.max(b))
   shifted = float(n)*(b - bmax)
   # to avoid under/overflow, maybe lam_shift = lam * shifted; if min < -700 underflow ok
   if lam > 0:
      w = np.exp(lam * shifted)
   else:
      w = np.ones(L)
   ws = float(np.sum(w))
   if not math.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0:
      w = np.zeros(L)
      w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
   else:
      w /= ws
   # cross terms
   w_rev = w[::-1]
   Ar = np.fft.rfft(a,Ngrad)
   Wr = np.fft.rfft(w_rev,Ngrad)
   C = np.fft.irfft(Ar*Wr,Ngrad)
   terms = C[idx]
   terms = np.where(np.isfinite(terms), terms, 0.0)
   grad_n = 2.0*float(n)*terms
   grad_n = np.where(np.isfinite(grad_n), grad_n, 0.0)
   grad_x = a*grad_n
   grad_x = np.where(np.isfinite(grad_x), grad_x, 0.0)
   return cmax, grad_x
```
For n=1, idx=arange(0,-? handle). For n=1, L=1, C length1, terms=C[idx0]. Good.

`_optimize_sequence(a0,n,seed,time_limit,lam,lr,id)`:
Need if n<=1 return. For n=1 score always2; skip.
```
def _optimize_sequence(a0, n, seed, time_limit, lam=20.0, lr=0.3, tag=""):
    if n < 2 or time_limit <= 0:
       return a0, 2.0
    a = _normalize_nonnegative(a0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    Nconv = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
    Ngrad = _next_pow2(3*n-2)
    if n == 1: idx = np.array([0]) else idx = np.arange(2*n-2, n-2, -1)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)
    # initial exact FFT score
    maxc0 = _seq_max_c(a,n,Nconv)
    best_score = 2.0 * maxc0
    best_a = a.copy()
    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    x = x - float(np.mean(x))
    m = np.zeros(n); v = np.zeros(n)
    t = 0
    lam_cur = float(lam)
    lr_cur = float(lr)
    no_improve = 0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       maxc, grad_x = _score_and_grad(a,n,lam_cur,Nconv,Ngrad,idx)
       if not math.isfinite(maxc):
           break
       score = 2.0*maxc
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
          lr_cur = min(1.2, lr_cur*1.005)
       else:
          no_improve += 1
          if no_improve > 300:
             lr_cur = max(0.05, lr_cur*0.9)
             if no_improve > 800:
                lam_cur = min(200.0, lam_cur*1.3)
       # Adam
       t +=1
       beta1=0.9; beta2=0.95
       m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*grad_x
       v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*(grad_x*grad_x)
       bc1 = 1 - beta1**t
       bc2 = 1 - beta2**t
       mh = m / max(bc1,1e-12)
       vh = v / max(bc2,1e-12)
       step = lr_cur * mh / (np.sqrt(vh) + 1e-12)
       x = x - step
       if it % 100 == 99:
           sigma = 0.1 if no_improve < 1000 else 0.2
           x = x + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
       x = x - float(np.mean(x))
       x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
       a = np.exp(x - float(np.max(x)))
       s = float(np.sum(a))
       if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
           a = best_a.copy(); x = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); x-=x.mean()
       else:
           a /= s
       it +=1
       if no_improve > 3000 and it % 500 == 0:
           # large restart around best: perturb best
           # maybe break
           break
    # final score exact FFT
    maxc_best = _seq_max_c(best_a,n,Nconv)
    best_score = 2.0*maxc_best
    best_a = _normalize_nonnegative(best_a)
    return best_a, float(best_score)
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-15.0)
    n = _choose_n()

    best_score = 2.0
    def? no closures. Use helper `_update_best`? no closures; just inline.

    big_starts.append(_template_kind(n,1,rng))
    big_starts.append(_template_kind(n,3,rng))

    # remove duplicates? not needed
    final_big_deadline = deadline - 180.0
    for i,a0 in enumerate(big_starts):
       if time.monotonic() >= final_big_deadline: break
       slice_s = max(30.0, min(600.0, (final_big_deadline-time.monotonic())/max(1,len(big_starts)-i)))
       a_opt, sc = _optimize_sequence(a0, n, seed+2000+i, slice_s, lam=20.0, lr=0.4, tag="big")
       if sc < best_score:
          best_a=a_opt.copy(); best_score=sc; print
    # final refine global

    if remain > 20:
       a_opt, sc = _optimize_sequence(best_a, n, seed+5000, remain, lam=50.0, lr=0.2, tag="final")
       if sc < best_score: update
    # exact candidates
    exact_candidates = []
    exact_candidates.append(_scale_for_return(best_a))

    exact_candidates.append(_scale_for_return(_template_kind(n,1,np.random.default_rng(seed+1))))
    exact_candidates.append(_scale_for_return(_template_kind(n,3, np.random.default_rng(seed+2))))

       # print

Need `_scale_for_return(a)`:
```
def _scale_for_return(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return [1.0]
    mx=float(np.max(a))
    if mx>0:
       scale = 1.0/mx  # max 1
       a = a*scale
       a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    # ensure sum >0.01: if sum <0.01 scale up maybe to 0.02/max? But max1 sum usually >0.01 for n>1. If n=1, a=[1]
    if a.sum() < 0.01:
       a = a * 1000.0
       a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

def _exact_score(seq):
   try:
      val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
      val=float(val)
   except Exception:
      return math.inf
   return val if math.isfinite(val) else math.inf

def _choose_n():
    default=4806
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    n=default
    try:
       if parent is not None:
          l=len(parent)
          if isinstance(l,int) and 1 <= l <= 8000:
             n=l
    except Exception:
       pass
    if n < 2: n=default
    if n > 8000: n=default
    return int(n)
```

Potential issue: In cross terms, we use Ngrad >= n + L -1 =3n-2. If pow2 >, zero padding, linear conv. We reverse w. Check formula again: terms_j = ∑ a_i w_{i+j}. Let conv(a, rev_w) index L-1-j. `C = conv(a, rev_w)`. For j=0, index L-1 = 2n-2. C[2n-2]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_i rev_w[2n-2-i] =∑ a_i w[L-1-(2n-2-i)] = w[i] because L-1=2n-2. Good j=0: ∑a_i w_i. For union? Wait for j=0 desired ∑a_i w_i (weights at same index i). But active lag k: grad_j = 2∑_{k} w_k a_{k-j}. Let i=k-j => k=i+j. For j=0, k=i => ∑ a_i w_i. Good. For j=1, index L-1-1=2n-3: C=∑ a_i rev_w[2n-3-i]=∑ a_i w[2n-2 - (2n-3-i)] =∑ a_i w[i+1] (where valid). Good. So terms slice indices descending from L-1 to L-n. ```python
"""
Search for non-negative step sequences minimizing the supplied evaluator.

For the real-valued non-negative objective, the evaluator is
    2 n max_k (a * a)_k / (sum a)^2
where a*a is the ordinary linear convolution.  This is scale invariant.

The algorithm:
  * works with sequences normalized to sum 1,
  * uses several asymmetric template starts,
  * refines them by a Fourier-space smooth max surrogate with Adam on the
    log-softmax parameters,
  * first optimizes the shape at a smaller length, interpolates it back,
    then refines at the final length,
  * finally validates the best few candidates with the exact evaluator and
    returns the best one.

A valid constant sequence is always the fallback and gives the value 2.0.
"""

import math
import time
import numpy as np


_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 4806
_MAX_RETURN_LENGTH = 6000


def _next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    """Next power of two >= max(1, m)."""
    x = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def _choose_length() -> int:
    """Prefer the known construction length when practical, else default."""
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    try:
        if parent is not None:
            ln = len(parent)
            if isinstance(ln, int) and 1 <= ln <= _MAX_RETURN_LENGTH:
                n = int(ln)
    except Exception:
        pass
    if n < 2:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    if n > _MAX_RETURN_LENGTH:
        n = _DEFAULT_LENGTH
    return int(n)


def _normalize_nonnegative(a) -> np.ndarray:
    """Convert to a finite non-negative 1D float array with sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if a.size == 0 or s <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones(_DEFAULT_LENGTH, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a / s
    return a


def _template_kind(n: int, kind: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
    """A collection of non-negative normalized templates."""
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    eps = 1e-12

    if kind == 0:
        a = 1.0 + eps
    elif kind == 1:
        a = 1.0 - t + eps
    elif kind == 2:
        a = (1.0 - t) ** 0.8 + eps
    elif kind == 3:
        a = (1.0 - t) ** 1.0 + eps
    elif kind == 4:
        a = (1.0 - t) ** 1.4 + eps
    elif kind == 5:
        a = np.exp(-1.2 * t) + eps
    elif kind == 6:
        a = np.exp(-3.0 * t) + eps
    elif kind == 7:
        a = np.exp(-8.0 * t) + eps
    elif kind == 8:
        a = 1.0 + 0.45 * np.cos(np.pi * t) + eps
    elif kind == 9:
        a = np.exp(-2.5 * t) + 0.20 * np.exp(-2.5 * (1.0 - t)) + eps
    elif kind == 10:
        a = 1.0 + np.tanh(2.0 * (0.6 - t)) + eps
    elif kind == 11:
        a = (1.0 - t) + 0.20 * np.exp(-5.0 * (1.0 - t)) + eps
    elif kind == 12:
        a = (1.0 - t) * (1.0 + 0.35 * np.sin(np.pi * t)) + eps
    else:
        w = max(1, int(n) // 64)
        kernel = np.ones(w, dtype=np.float64)
        g = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, int(n))
        a = np.abs(np.convolve(g, kernel, mode="same")) / float(w) + eps

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if a.size == 0 or s <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a / s
    return a


def _seq_max_c(a: np.ndarray, n: int, n_fft: int = None) -> float:
    """
    Return max_k (a*a)_k * n for sum(a)=1.
    The evaluator score is 2 * max_k (a*a)_k * n / sum(a)^2,
    so for sum=1 it is 2 * this value.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return math.inf
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if n_fft is None:
        n_fft = _next_pow2(L)

    A = np.fft.rfft(a, n_fft)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, n_fft)
    b = b[:L]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b)
    np.nan_to_num(b, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)

    if b.size == 0:
        return math.inf
    return float(np.max(b)) * float(n)


def _score_and_grad(
    a: np.ndarray,
    n: int,
    lam: float,
    n_conv_fft: int,
    n_grad_fft: int,
    idx
) -> tuple[float, np.ndarray]:
    """
    Compute scaled max coefficient and softmax-space gradient of a
    log-sum-exp surrogate for max(a*a).

    Returns:
      max_c : max_k (a*a)_k * n
      grad_v : gradient with respect to x where a = softmax(x)
    """
    L = 2 * n - 1

    A = np.fft.rfft(a, n_conv_fft)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, n_conv_fft)
    b = b[:L]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b)
    np.nan_to_num(b, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)

    bmax = float(np.max(b))
    max_c = bmax * float(n)
    if not math.isfinite(max_c) or bmax <= 0.0:
        return math.inf, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    shifted = bmax * (b / bmax - 1.0)
    if lam > 0.0:
        w = np.exp(lam * shifted)
    else:
        w = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)

    np.nan_to_num(w, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if not math.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= ws

    # terms[j] = sum_i a_i * w_{i+j}
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    Ar = np.fft.rfft(a, n_grad_fft)
    Wr = np.fft.rfft(w_rev, n_grad_fft)
    C = np.fft.irfft(Ar * Wr, n_grad_fft)
    terms = C[idx]

    terms = np.asarray(terms, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if terms.size != n:
        terms = np.resize(terms, n)
    np.nan_to_num(terms, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    # derivative wrt a of the surrogate:
    # d/d a_j sum_k w_k * n * (a*a)_k = 2 n sum_i a_i w_{i+j}
    grad_a = 2.0 * float(n) * terms
    np.nan_to_num(grad_a, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    # softmax-chain gradient:
    # a = softmax(x), grad_x = a_j * (grad_a_j - sum_l a_l grad_a_l)
    mean_grad = float(np.dot(a, grad_a))
    grad_v = a * (grad_a - mean_grad)
    np.nan_to_num(grad_v, copy=False, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    return max_c, grad_v


def _optimize_sequence(
    a0: np.ndarray,
    n: int,
    seed: int,
    time_limit: float,
    lam: float = 25.0,
    lr: float = 0.6,
    tag: str = ""
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, float]:
    """
    Optimize one start at fixed length n.  The returned score is the FFT
    estimate of the evaluator score for sum(a)=1.
    """
    a = _normalize_nonnegative(a0)
    if n < 2 or time_limit <= 0.0:
        mc = _seq_max_c(a, n)
        return a, float(2.0 * mc)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n_conv_fft = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    n_grad_fft = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    if n == 1:
        idx = np.array([0], dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        # indices 2n-2, 2n-3, ..., n
        idx = np.arange(2 * n - 2, n - 2, -1, dtype=np.int64)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max(0.0, time_limit))

    mc0 = _seq_max_c(a, n, n_conv_fft)
    best_score = float(2.0 * mc0)
    best_a = a.copy()

    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    x = x - float(np.mean(x))
    x = x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n)
    x = x - float(np.mean(x))
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0

    lam_cur = float(max(1.0, lam))
    lr_cur = float(max(0.01, lr))
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        mc, grad_v = _score_and_grad(a, n, lam_cur, n_conv_fft, n_grad_fft, idx)
        if not math.isfinite(mc):
            break

        score = float(2.0 * mc)

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            lr_cur = min(1.5, lr_cur * 1.004)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 400:
                lr_cur = max(0.05, lr_cur * 0.92)
            if no_improve > 1200:
                lam_cur = min(220.0, lam_cur * 1.35)

        if tag:
            if it % 2000 == 0:
                print(
                    "opt[%s] n=%d it=%d lam=%.2f lr=%.4f score=%.10f best=%.10f"
                    % (tag, n, it, lam_cur, lr_cur, score, best_score),
                    flush=True
                )

        gnorm = float(np.max(np.abs(grad_v))) if grad_v.size else 0.0
        if not math.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-16:
            if no_improve > 800:
                break
            x = x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, n)
            x = x - float(np.mean(x))
            x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
            it += 1
            continue

        # Adam
        beta1 = 0.90
        beta2 = 0.95
        t += 1
        m = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * grad_v
        v = beta2 * v + (1.0 - beta2) * (grad_v * grad_v)

        bc1 = 1.0 - (beta1 ** t)
        bc2 = 1.0 - (beta2 ** t)
        if bc1 <= 1e-15:
            bc1 = 1e-15
        if bc2 <= 1e-15:
            bc2 = 1e-15

        mh = m / bc1
        vh = v / bc2
        step = lr_cur * mh / (np.sqrt(vh) + 1e-12)

        x = x - step

        if it % 120 == 119:
            sigma = 0.08 if no_improve < 800 else 0.18
            x = x + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)

        x = x - float(np.mean(x))
        x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)

        a = np.exp(x - float(np.max(x)))
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            a = best_a.copy()
            x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
            x = x - float(np.mean(x))
            x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 50.0)
        else:
            a = a / s

        it += 1

        if no_improve > 4000:
            break

    mc_best = _seq_max_c(best_a, n, n_conv_fft)
    if not math.isfinite(mc_best):
        return best_a, best_score
    return best_a, float(2.0 * mc_best)


def _scale_for_return(a: np.ndarray) -> list[float]:
    """Scale a search sequence for return: max 1, non-negative, finite."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0]

    mx = float(np.max(a))
    if mx > 0.0:
        a = a / mx
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        a = a * 1000.0
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    return [float(x) for x in a]


def _exact_score(seq: list[float]) -> float:
    """Safely call the injected exact evaluator."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return math.inf
    return val if math.isfinite(val) else math.inf


def propose_candidate(seed: int = 42, budget_s: float = 2400.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Return a practical best-effort non-negative sequence.
    """
    try:
        t_start = time.monotonic()
        remaining_budget = float(max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0))
        deadline = t_start + remaining_budget

        n = _choose_length()
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

        # Fallback optimal under the displayed evaluator: constant has score 2.
        best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_fft_score = 2.0
        print("initial constant: n=%d fft_score=%.10f" % (n, best_fft_score), flush=True)

        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        # Stage 1: small-length shape search.
        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        small_n = int(min(n, 1024))
        if small_n < 64:
            small_n = int(max(16, n))

        total_time = max(1.0, deadline - t_start)
        small_deadline = t_start + min(360.0, max(30.0, 0.25 * total_time))
        small_deadline = min(small_deadline, deadline - 240.0)
        if small_deadline <= t_start:
            small_deadline = t_start

        small_death_shot = [1, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 2]
        for si, kind in enumerate(small_death_shot):
            if time.monotonic() >= small_deadline:
                break
            a_small = _template_kind(small_n, kind, rng)
            slice_s = max(
                2.0,
                min(
                    60.0,
                    (small_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(max(1, len(small_death_shot) - si))
                )
            )
            a_small, sc_small = _optimize_sequence(
                a_small, small_n, int(seed + 1000 * (si + 1)), slice_s,
                lam=35.0, lr=0.8, tag="small"
            )

            if n != small_n:
                tb = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
                ts = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, small_n, dtype=np.float64)
                a_big = np.interp(tb, ts, a_small)
            else:
                a_big = a_small.copy()
            a_big = _normalize_nonnegative(a_big)

            mc = _seq_max_c(a_big, n)
            sc = float(2.0 * mc)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc
                best_a = a_big.copy()
                print(
                    "shape improved: kind=%d small_n=%d big_n=%d fft_score=%.10f"
                    % (kind, small_n, n, best_fft_score),
                    flush=True
                )

        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        # Stage 2: full-length multi-start refinement.
        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        full_deadline = deadline - 240.0
        if full_deadline <= time.monotonic():
            full_deadline = time.monotonic()

        full_starts = []
        full_starts.append(best_a.copy())
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 1, rng))
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 3, rng))
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 5, rng))
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 6, rng))
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 11, rng))
        full_starts.append(_template_kind(n, 12, rng))

        for fi, a0 in enumerate(full_starts):
            if time.monotonic() >= full_deadline:
                break
            slice_s = max(
                30.0,
                min(
                    420.0,
                    (full_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(max(1, len(full_starts) - fi))
                )
            )
            a_opt, sc = _optimize_sequence(
                a0, n, int(seed + 20000 + 100 * fi), slice_s,
                lam=25.0, lr=0.6, tag="full"
            )
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc
                best_a = a_opt.copy()
                print(
                    "full improved: start=%d n=%d fft_score=%.10f"
                    % (fi, n, best_fft_score),
                    flush=True
                )

        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        # Stage 3: final refinement of the current global best.
        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        final_deadline = deadline - 60.0
        if final_deadline > time.monotonic() + 15.0:
            slice_s = final_deadline - time.monotonic()
            a_opt, sc = _optimize_sequence(
                best_a, n, int(seed + 90000), slice_s,
                lam=55.0, lr=0.35, tag="final"
            )
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc
                best_a = a_opt.copy()
            print(
                "final fft best: n=%d fft_score=%.10f elapsed=%.1f"
                % (n, best_fft_score, time.monotonic() - t_start),
                flush=True
            )

        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        # Exact validation of a few strong candidates.
        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        candidates = []
        candidates.append(_scale_for_return(best_a))
        candidates.append([1.0] * n)
        candidates.append(_scale_for_return(_template_kind(n, 1, np.random.default_rng(seed + 777))))
        candidates.append(_scale_for_return(_template_kind(n, 3, np.random.default_rng(seed + 778))))
        candidates.append(_scale_for_return(_template_kind(n, 6, np.random.default_rng(seed + 779))))

        best_seq = [1.0] * n
        best_exact = math.inf

        for qi, seq in enumerate(candidates):
            sc = _exact_score(seq)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact:
                best_exact = sc
                best_seq = seq
            print(
                "exact candidate %d: score=%.12f" % (qi, sc),
                flush=True
            )

        if not math.isfinite(best_exact):
            return [1.0] * n

        return best_seq

    except Exception:
        # Absolute fallback: valid, non-negative, and exact score 2.
        return [1.0] * _DEFAULT_LENGTH
```
